Answer Reliability Audit

Internal consistency report for the corrected local question bank. This flags likely wrong keys; it is not a substitute for clinician/faculty review of every medical fact.

Conflict groups summary

Face to pubis delivery is possible with which cephalic presentation

Records: exams_q20, exams_q45

Answer texts: mento anterior | occipito posterior

Most important symptom of undisturbed ectopic pregnancy

Records: exams_q21, exams_q46

Answer texts: abdominal pain | amenorrhea

A pregnant lady in the 10th week of gestation. Which of the following is not included in the educational message for her?

Records: exams_q53, exams_q121

Answer texts: promotion for breastfeeding | labor signs and onset of labor

The following are predisposing factors for placenta previa, EXCEPT

Records: exams_q90, final_41_mcq1

Answer texts: malpresentation | malpresentsation

Your patient has microcytic anemia with a hemoglobin of 9 and normal iron stores. What is the most likely diagnosis?

Records: exams_q99, exams_q112, quizzes_q41, quizzes_q153, other_q175

Answer texts: thalassemia | folate deficiency

The placenta of twins can be

Records: exams_q110, exams_q127, quizzes_q212, other_q134

Answer texts: monochorionic and diamniotic in monozygotic twins | mono chorionic and di amniotic in monozygotic twins

All of the following are maternal changes during pregnancy EXCEPT

Records: exams_q111, exams_q128, quizzes_q55, quizzes_q226, other_q118

Answer texts: increase alveolar residual volume | increased fibrinogen | increased cardiac output | increased alveolar residual volume

Ritgen maneuver is done in

Records: exams_q115, quizzes_q20, other_q188

Answer texts: for delivery of the head in normal labor | for delivery of the head in breech presentation

A patient of 36 weeks gestation presents with hypertension, blurring of vision and headache. Her blood pressure reading was persistent 180/120. Proteinuria +3. How will you manage this patient?

Records: exams_q135, quizzes_q220

Answer texts: admit the patient start antihypertensive mgso4 and terminate the pregnancy | admit the patient start antihypertensive mgso₄ and terminate the pregnancy

A 25 years old female, Gravida 5, Para 0+3, with history of recurrent early pregnancy loss. Which of the following investigations is NOT ordered?

Records: exams_q144, quizzes_q60, quizzes_q149

Answer texts: testing for torch | testing for antiphospholipid syndrome

Ventouse in second stage of labor is contraindicated in

Records: exams_q151, quizzes_q34, quizzes_q80, quizzes_q148, quizzes_q246, other_q127, final_41_mcq20

Answer texts: preterm labor | heart disease | persistent occipito posterior position

True about frank breech

Records: quizzes_q12, quizzes_q99, quizzes_q168, quizzes_q193, other_q186

Answer texts: thigh flexed knee extended | thigh extended knee extended | both are flexed

The following techniques are used for delivery of the aftercoming head in breech presentation except

Records: quizzes_q14, quizzes_q102, quizzes_q171, quizzes_q189

Answer texts: lovsets maneuver | burns marshall technique | jaw flexion shoulder traction | mauriceau smillie veit technique

Records: quizzes_q16, quizzes_q134, other_q144

Answer texts: brow | vertex

Records: quizzes_q18, quizzes_q54, quizzes_q132, other_q185

Answer texts: direct oxytocin injection after delivery of the shoulders | immediate cutting and cord clamping

What is the measurement of sub-occipito-bregmatic diameter?

Records: quizzes_q19, quizzes_q50, quizzes_q138

Answer texts: 105 cm | 95 cm | 9 cm

All of the following are true regarding cephalohematoma EXCEPT

Records: quizzes_q31, quizzes_q74, quizzes_q107, quizzes_q172, quizzes_q197, quizzes_q243, other_q124

Answer texts: disappears within two days | normal overlying skin | develops hours after birth | well defined edges | localized over one bone

Records: quizzes_q33, quizzes_q75, quizzes_q114

Answer texts: two weeks | three weeks

Answer texts: uterus contracting | station 0 or +1

Records: quizzes_q44, other_q172b

Answer texts: decreased fibrinogen | decreased thyroxin binding globulin

All of the following are sure signs of pregnancy EXCEPT

Records: quizzes_q46, other_q77, other_q82, other_q157, other_q201

Answer texts: quickening | umbilical soufflé | uterine soufflé

A primigravida woman with mitral stenosis with no pulmonary hypertension is in labor at 39 weeks. She has dyspnea only in severe exertion. Fetus is in cephalic presentation. Cervix dilatation is 5 cm. She has two uterine contractions in 10 minutes. Which of the following should be avoided in this patient

Records: quizzes_q48, quizzes_q118, quizzes_q157

Answer texts: use of epidural analgesia for pain relief | ventouse delivery in the second stage of labor if delivery does not occur in half hour | use of ergometrine in the third stage of labor

All of the following are true about pathological retraction ring EXCEPT

Records: quizzes_q51, quizzes_q98, quizzes_q140, quizzes_q167, quizzes_q191

Answer texts: can be relieved by anesthesia | can be felt | associated with maternal dehydration | may be associated with intrauterine fetal death | occurs between upper and lower uterine segment

A primigravida complains of lower abdominal pain at a period of amenorrhea of 32 weeks. The fetus is in cephalic presentation. She has 1-2 uterine contractions per 10 minutes. Cervix is 2 cm dilated and 75% effaced with intact membranes. What is the preferred next step of management?

Records: quizzes_q52, quizzes_q64, quizzes_q209, other_q163

Answer texts: start intravenous broad spectrum antibiotics | commence intramuscular corticosteroids and oral nifedipine

A woman complains of right sided lower abdominal pain and mild vaginal bleeding after a period of amenorrhea of 6 weeks. Her general condition is satisfactory. Serum hCG is 2200 IU/ml. Transvaginal ultrasound reveals adnexal mass 20 mm with no cardiac activity, and no intrauterine pregnancy. The most appropriate management is:

Records: quizzes_q59, quizzes_q223, other_q115

Answer texts: treat with intramuscular methotrexate | treat with intramuscular methyl ergometrine

The following are related to uterine soufflé EXCEPT

Records: quizzes_q62, quizzes_q227, other_q120

Answer texts: it is due to increased blood flow through the umbilical artery | it is a soft blowing systolic murmur heard on the sides of the pregnant uterus

With reference to fetal heart rate, a non-stress test is considered reactive when

Records: quizzes_q63, quizzes_q90, quizzes_q207, other_q168

Answer texts: two fetal heart rate accelerations are noted in 20 minutes | two fetal heart rate accelerations are noted in 10 minutes | three heart rate accelerations are noted in 30 minutes

Monozygotic twins have the following characteristics EXCEPT

Records: quizzes_q65, quizzes_q91, quizzes_q204, other_q164

Answer texts: has different blood groups | more susceptible to twin to twin transfusion syndrome | accounts for 20% of twin pregnancies

All of the following maneuvers are performed in shoulder dystocia EXCEPT

Records: quizzes_q66, quizzes_q92, quizzes_q205, other_q165

Answer texts: fundal pressure | mcroberts position | episiotomy

Records: quizzes_q67, quizzes_q94, quizzes_q202, other_q160

Answer texts: absent uterine artery diastolic notch | increased maternal serum alpha fetoprotein

Records: quizzes_q69, quizzes_q89, quizzes_q206, other_q167

Answer texts: duodenal atresia | post maturity | iugr

G2 para 1+0 who had uncomplicated lower segment cesarean section in her first pregnancy in labor. She is allowed a trial of vaginal delivery. What is the earliest warning sign of scar rupture?

Records: quizzes_q76, quizzes_q147, quizzes_q250, other_q131

Answer texts: persistent mento posterior | maternal heart disease | deep transverse arrest

Regarding indications of cesarean hysterectomy, which of the following statements is NOT true

Answer texts: placenta accreta encountered during cesarean section | the mere presence of couvelaire uterus | uncontrolled postpartum hemorrhage

A woman complains of right-sided abdominal pain and mild vaginal bleeding for one day after a period of amenorrhea of 6 weeks. Her general condition is satisfactory. There is tenderness in the right iliac fossa. The urine pregnancy test is positive. Transvaginal ultrasound scan does not reveal any adnexal mass or intrauterine pregnancy. What is the next step of management?

Records: quizzes_q81, quizzes_q215, other_q107

Answer texts: repeat ultrasound scan after 7 days | perform two serum beta hcg tests 46 hours apart

Answer texts: umbilical souffle | umbilical soufflé | auscultation of fetal heart sounds fihs

Answer texts: severe abdominal adhesions during cs | severe chorioamnionitis

Application of forceps is appropriate in which of the following situations?

Records: quizzes_q113, quizzes_q178, quizzes_q194

Answer texts: vertex at +3 station cervix 8 cm dilated membranes ruptured | mentum anterior +3 station cervix completely dilated membranes ruptured

A hypertensive pregnant woman at 34 weeks comes with history of abdominal pain, vaginal bleeding and loss of fetal movement. On examination the uterus is contracted with increased uterine tone. Fetal heart rate is absent. The most likely diagnosis is

Records: quizzes_q125, quizzes_q230, other_q133

Answer texts: abruptio placenta | abruption placenta

Mrs H, Gravida 2 para 1+0, presented to hospital in labor pains, on examination she had 3 uterine contractions of 20 seconds in 10 minutes, cervical dilatation 10 cm with bearing down and FHR 145 per minute. What is the stage of labor?

Records: quizzes_q165, quizzes_q192

Answer texts: stage i | stage ii

The uterus becomes pelvic organ after delivery by

Records: quizzes_q174, quizzes_q201, quizzes_q245, other_q126

Answer texts: three weeks | two weeks

Which of the following is NOT a component of premarital care?

Records: other_q1, other_q16

Answer texts: inter conception care | promotion for proper anc

Which of the following methods is the correct way to calculate the estimated date of delivery (EDD)?

Records: other_q3, other_q18, other_q96

Answer texts: first day of last menstrual period lmp + 8 months + 1 week | first day of last menstrual period lmp + 3 months + 1 week

At what stage of gestation would you expect a multiparous woman to begin to feel fetal movements?

Records: other_q6, other_q21, other_q103

Answer texts: 16 18 weeks | at 16 18 weeks | at 16 weeks

Which is a contraindication for forceps delivery?

Records: other_q52, other_q223

Answer texts: direct mento posterior position | direct mentoposterior position

Likely OKquizzes_q1Quizzes#1

A woman is admitted at 36 weeks gestation following sudden episode of antepartum haemorrhage. The bleeding has ceased on admission. She is not pale. BP 120/80 and pulse is 76 beats/minute. The uterus is soft and not tender. There are no palpable uterine contractions. FHR is 140/minute. What is the next step of management?

A Ultrasound examination of the uterus
B Examine the cervix using Cusco's speculum
C Perform cardiotocograph
D Pelvic examination under general anesthesia
E Administer intravenous dexamethasone
Current answer: A) Ultrasound examination of the uterus
Justification

* Painless APH at 36w with soft, non-tender uterus suggests placenta previa. US to locate placenta before any vaginal exam.

Likely OKquizzes_q2Quizzes#2

This image shows one of the maneuvers used in shoulder dystocia:

A Internal rotation
B Zavanelli maneuver
C Delivery of posterior arm
D Screw maneuver
Current answer: C) Delivery of posterior arm
Justification

Likely OKquizzes_q3Quizzes#3

A pregnant woman is found to have excessive accumulation of amniotic fluid. Such polyhydramnios is likely to be associated with all of the following conditions EXCEPT

A Twins pregnancy
B Oesophageal atresia
C Microcephaly
D Bilateral renal agenesis
E Open spina bifida
Current answer: C) Microcephaly
Justification

* Microcephaly can be associated with polyhydramnios (impaired swallowing). Bilateral renal agenesis is the EXCEPT here — it causes oligohydramnios, so it is correctly associated with oligohydramnios, not polyhydramnios.

Likely OKquizzes_q4Quizzes#4

A woman with dichorionic diamniotic twins attends antenatal clinic at 36 weeks. The first twin is in breech presentation and the second is in cephalic presentation. She has no other pregnancy complications. What is the most appropriate management?

A Perform internal podalic version
B Await spontaneous onset of labor
C Induce labor at 37 weeks
D Perform external cephalic version of the first
E Perform elective cesarean section at 37 weeks
Current answer: B) Await spontaneous onset of labor
Justification

* DCDA twins with cephalic first twin at 36 weeks without complications: awaiting spontaneous labour is appropriate. Elective CS at 37 weeks is not indicated.

Likely OKquizzes_q5Quizzes#5

All predispose to isoimmunization in Rh-negative female EXCEPT

A Ectopic pregnancy
B Advanced maternal age
C Vaginal delivery
D Cesarean section
E Antepartum hemorrhage
Current answer: B) Advanced maternal age
Justification

* Rh isoimmunization requires fetomaternal hemorrhage (FMH). Ectopic pregnancy, vaginal delivery, C-section, and APH all cause FMH. Advanced age alone does not.

Likely OKquizzes_q6Quizzes#6

A Abruptio placenta
B Amniotic fluid embolism
C Chorioamnionitis
D Rupture uterus
E Red degeneration of fibroid
Current answer: A) Abruptio placenta
Justification

* Classic concealed abruption: sudden severe abdominal pain, shock, tender/woody uterus, no vaginal bleeding. Not in labor.

Likely OKquizzes_q7Quizzes#7

* First step in suspected PPROM is sterile speculum to confirm pooling, ferning, nitrazine test before any other intervention.

Likely OKquizzes_q9Quizzes#9

A Amniotic fluid volume
B Oxytocin challenge test
C Fetal respiratory movement
D Non-stress test
E Fetal body movement
Current answer: B) Oxytocin challenge test
Justification

* Biophysical profile (BPP) components: NST, fetal breathing movements, fetal body movements, fetal tone, and amniotic fluid volume. Oxytocin challenge test (CST) is separate.

Likely OKquizzes_q10Quizzes#10

D Place cervical cerclage
E Administer magnesium sulphate
Current answer: C) Administer antibiotics
Justification

* PPROM at 35w with fever (38°C), tachycardia (102) = clinical chorioamnionitis. Requires prompt antibiotics and delivery.

Likely OKquizzes_q11Quizzes#11

In which presentation the fetal head is deflexed?

A Compound presentation
B Face presentation
C Occipito-posterior position
D Occipito-anterior position
E Brow presentation
Current answer: E) Brow presentation
Justification

* Brow presentation is the most deflexed presentation (the head is between full flexion and full extension). Occipito-posterior can have some deflexion but brow is the most deflexed.

Conflict reviewquizzes_q16Quizzes#16

Likely OKquizzes_q17Quizzes#17

Treatment of postpartum hemorrhage includes all EXCEPT:

A Oxytocin
B Syntometrine
C Oestrogen
D Carbetocin
Current answer: C) Oestrogen
Justification

* Estrogen has no role in management of PPH. Oxytocin, syntometrine (oxytocin + ergometrine), and carbetocin are all uterotonics.

Likely OKquizzes_q21Quizzes#21

All of the following are physiological changes during pregnancy EXCEPT

A Increased tidal volume
B Decreased thyroxin-binding globulin
C Increased glomerular filtration rate
D Increased fibrinogen
E Increased stroke volume
Current answer: B) Decreased thyroxin-binding globulin
Justification

* TBG increases in pregnancy due to estrogen stimulation. Tidal volume, GFR, fibrinogen, and stroke volume all increase.

Likely OKquizzes_q22Quizzes#22

A Monitor till HCG is zero
B Insertion of intrauterine device (IUD)
C Methotrexate
D Hysterectomy
E Follow up at 1 year

Justification

* Persistent elevated beta-HCG 3 months after molar evacuation indicates post-molar GTN. First-line treatment is methotrexate chemotherapy.

Likely OKquizzes_q23Quizzes#23

A woman is admitted to the antenatal ward at 35 weeks. Her blood pressure is 179/110 and the urine contains albumin. She has generalized seizure during examination. An oropharyngeal airway is inserted. What is the most appropriate next step in management?

A Admit to the intensive care unit
B Give 10 mg of intravenous hydralazine
C Immediate transfer for cesarean section
D Insert an indwelling catheter
E Give 4 grams bolus of magnesium sulphate
Current answer: E) Give 4 grams bolus of magnesium sulphate
Justification

* Eclampsia with active seizure: MgSO4 (4g IV bolus) is the drug of choice for seizure control and prevention of recurrence.

Likely OKquizzes_q24Quizzes#24

Likely OKquizzes_q25Quizzes#25

In case of diabetes mellitus with pregnancy:

A The best screening test is urine glucose
B Congenital malformations are unrelated to diabetic control
C Insulin requirement decreases as pregnancy advances
D There is increased incidence of hydramnios and preeclampsia
E Oral hypoglycemics are preferred to insulin therapy
Current answer: D) There is increased incidence of hydramnios and preeclampsia
Justification

* Gestational diabetes increases risk of both polyhydramnios and preeclampsia. Urine glucose is unreliable, malformations relate to glycemic control, insulin need increases, and insulin is preferred over OHA.

Reviewquizzes_q26Quizzes#26

The time at which cardiac output is highest during pregnancy is:

A In the first trimester
B In the second trimester
C In the third trimester
D During labor
E Immediately postpartum
Current answer: E) Immediately postpartum
Why flagged: Justification appears to mention a different option label.
Justification

* Rh immunoglobulin (anti-D) is given within 72 hours postpartum to prevent Rh sensitisation in Rh-negative mothers with an Rh-positive baby.

Likely OKquizzes_q27Quizzes#27

All of the following are structures of the fetal membranes EXCEPT

A Decidua
B Allantois
C Yolk sac
D Connecting stalk
E Chorion
Current answer: A) Decidua
Justification

* Decidua is maternal endometrium, not part of the fetal membranes. The fetal membranes include chorion, amnion, yolk sac, allantois, and connecting stalk.

Likely OKquizzes_q28Quizzes#28

A primigravida woman with mitral stenosis without pulmonary hypertension is in labor at 39 weeks. She has dyspnea only on severe exertion. Fetus is in cephalic presentation, cervix is 5 cm dilated, and she has 2 uterine contractions in 10 minutes. Which of the following should be avoided in this patient?

A Use of epidural analgesia for pain relief
B Ventouse delivery if second stage prolonged
C Placing the patient in semi-sitting position
D Use of ergometrine in third stage of labor
E Active management of third stage of labor
Current answer: A) Use of epidural analgesia for pain relief
Justification

* Ergometrine causes vasoconstriction and increased venous return, risking pulmonary edema in heart disease. Otherwise, semi-sitting, epidural, shortening second stage, and active management are appropriate.

Likely OKquizzes_q29Quizzes#29

A woman presents with abdominal pain and nausea with amenorrhea for 5 weeks. Ectopic pregnancy is diagnosed if:

A Beta HCG > 1000 IU/L with endometrial thickness of 14 mm
B Beta HCG > 2000 IU/L with no gestational sac in uterus by transvaginal US
C Beta HCG < 1000 IU/L with empty uterus on transvaginal US
D Serum progesterone > 25 ng/dL
E Serum prolactin > 20 ng/dL
Current answer: B) Beta HCG > 2000 IU/L with no gestational sac in uterus by transvaginal US
Justification

* The discriminatory zone: when TVUS shows no IUP and beta-HCG > 2000 IU/L, ectopic pregnancy is very likely.

Likely OKquizzes_q30Quizzes#30

A patient has microcytic anemia with hemoglobin 9 and normal iron stores. What is the most likely diagnosis?

A Thalassemia
B Iron deficiency anemia
C Vitamin B6 deficiency
D Vitamin B12 deficiency
E Folate deficiency
Current answer: A) Thalassemia
Justification

* Microcytic anemia with normal iron stores is characteristic of thalassemia (impaired globin chain synthesis), not iron deficiency.

Conflict reviewquizzes_q31Quizzes#31

All of the following are true regarding cephalohematoma EXCEPT

A Develops hours after birth
B Well-defined edges
C Normal overlying skin
D Disappears within two days
E Localized over one bone
Current answer: D) Disappears within two days
Why flagged: Duplicate question has conflicting keyed answers.
Conflict group: quizzes_q31, quizzes_q74, quizzes_q107, quizzes_q172, quizzes_q197, quizzes_q243, other_q124
Majority answer text: disappears within two days
Justification

* Cephalohematoma (subperiosteal bleed) resolves over weeks (2–4 weeks), not days. It develops hours after birth, has well-defined edges, is localized over one bone, and overlying skin is normal.

Likely OKquizzes_q32Quizzes#32

G2 para 1+0 who had uncomplicated lower segment cesarean section in her first pregnancy is in labor. She is allowed a trial of vaginal delivery. What is the earliest warning sign of scar rupture?

A Fetal distress
B Fresh vaginal bleeding
C Severe continuous abdominal pain
D Maternal tachycardia
Current answer: A) Fetal distress
Justification

* Fetal heart rate abnormalities (bradycardia, decelerations) are the earliest warning sign of uterine scar rupture during TOLAC/VBAC.

Likely OKquizzes_q39Quizzes#39

D Uncontrolled postpartum hemorrhage
E Operable carcinoma of the cervix during pregnancy
Current answer: D) Uncontrolled postpartum hemorrhage
Justification

* Placenta accreta is a classic indication for cesarean hysterectomy. Couvelaire uterus alone is NOT an indication (it resolves). The question asks which statement is TRUE — placenta accreta is a true indication.

Likely OKquizzes_q49Quizzes#49

Conflict reviewquizzes_q51Quizzes#51

Likely wrongquizzes_q55Quizzes#55

All of the following are maternal changes during pregnancy EXCEPT

B Increased fibrinogen
C Increased cardiac output
D Increased alveolar residual volume
Current answer: B) Increased fibrinogen
Why flagged: Duplicate question has a different majority answer.
Conflict group: exams_q111, exams_q128, quizzes_q55, quizzes_q226, other_q118
Majority answer text: increase alveolar residual volume
Justification

* In pregnancy, the diaphragm is elevated, causing decreased (not increased) residual volume and functional residual capacity. GFR, fibrinogen, and cardiac output all increase.

Likely OKquizzes_q56Quizzes#56

Regarding the process of fertilization, which of the following statements is correct?

A Fertilization occurs most commonly in isthmus of the fallopian tube
B Implantation occurs most commonly on posterior wall near the fundus
C Implantation begins 14 days after fertilization
D Morula enters uterus on day 7
Current answer: B) Implantation occurs most commonly on posterior wall near the fundus
Justification

* Fertilization occurs in the ampulla (not isthmus). Implantation begins day 6–7 (not day 14). Morula enters uterus day 3–4 (not day 7). Implantation is most commonly posterior fundal.

Likely OKquizzes_q58Quizzes#58

A primigravida 32 years old female, admitted to the antenatal ward at 35 weeks, her BP was 160/110. Her urine does not contain albumin. Which of the following drugs should NOT be used in her management?

A Labetalol
B Methyldopa
C Furosemide
D Hydralazine
Current answer: C) Furosemide
Justification

* Diuretics like furosemide are avoided in pregnancy as they can reduce placental perfusion. Methyldopa, labetalol, and hydralazine are safe antihypertensives.

Likely wrongquizzes_q68Quizzes#68

The following abnormal findings can be found in IUGR baby EXCEPT

Current answer: C) Absent uterine artery diastolic notch
Why flagged: Duplicate question has a different majority answer.
Conflict group: quizzes_q68, quizzes_q211, other_q161
Majority answer text: increased maternal serum alpha fetoprotein
Justification

* In normal pregnancy, the uterine artery notch disappears by 24 weeks. Absence of notch is NORMAL. In IUGR, uterine artery notch often PERSISTS. Oligohydramnios, abnormal H/A ratio, and absent umbilical flow are abnormal IUGR findings.

Likely OKquizzes_q123Quizzes#123

Current answer: E) Inferior vena cava compression

Likely OKquizzes_q124Quizzes#124

D The patient has a urine infection
E The patient has diabetes
Current answer: C) The patient’s urinalysis is consistent with normal pregnancy
Justification

* Trace glucosuria is common in pregnancy due to increased GFR (~50%) and decreased renal glucose threshold; it is not diagnostic of diabetes.

Justification

* Caput succedaneum (scalp edema) resolves in 1–3 days, not 2 weeks. It has ill-defined edges, crosses sutures, develops at birth, and may have dark overlying skin.

Likely OKquizzes_q150Quizzes#150

A patient presents at 8 week's pregnant or at 8 week's gestation for her prenatal visit. Her main complaint is nausea and occasional vomiting. Her weight is the same as her prepregnancy weight. What do you tell her?

Current answer: E) She has delayed gastric emptying due to progesterone effect
Justification

* Progesterone slows GI motility, contributing to nausea. Nausea without weight loss at 8w is normal pregnancy, not hyperemesis.

Likely OKquizzes_q163Quizzes#163

Which of the following drugs is NOT used for tocolysis

A Phenobarbitone
B Indomethacin
C Nifedipine
D Magnesium sulphate
E Ritodrine
Current answer: A) Phenobarbitone
Justification

* Phenobarbitone is an anticonvulsant/barbiturate, NOT a tocolytic. Ritodrine (beta-agonist), indomethacin (NSAID), nifedipine (CCB), and MgSO4 are tocolytics.

* Lambda sign (twin peak sign) is seen in dichorionic diamniotic twins. The T-sign is seen in monochorionic diamniotic twins.

Likely OKquizzes_q203Quizzes#203

All of the following are ultrasound features of intrauterine fetal demise EXCEPT

A Absent fetal movement
B Collapse of the thorax
C Gases in the intestine
D Turtle sign
E Spalding sign
Current answer: D) Turtle sign
Justification

* Turtle sign (head retraction) is a clinical sign of shoulder dystocia, not an ultrasound feature of fetal demise. US features of demise: absent movement, collapsed thorax, gas in bowel, Spalding sign (overlapping skull bones).

Likely OKquizzes_q231Quizzes#231

All are causes of intrauterine growth restriction EXCEPT:

A Pregnancy induced hypertension
B Tubal ectopic pregnancy
C Maternal heart disease
D Anemia
Current answer: B) Tubal ectopic pregnancy
Justification

* Tubal ectopic pregnancy is not an intrauterine pregnancy and cannot cause IUGR. PIH, maternal heart disease, and anemia can all cause IUGR.

Likely OKquizzes_q232Quizzes#232

A pregnant woman is found to have excessive accumulation of amniotic fluid. Such polyhydramnios is likely to be associated with all of the following conditions EXCEPT:

A Twins pregnancy
B Bilateral renal agenesis
C Microcephaly
D Oesophageal atresia
Current answer: C) Microcephaly
Justification

* Same as Q3 — microcephaly can be associated with polyhydramnios (impaired swallowing centre/normal swallowing).

Likely OKquizzes_q233Quizzes#233

Complications of diabetes in pregnancy include all the following EXCEPT

A Intrauterine growth restriction (IUGR)
B Shoulder dystocia
C Hyperglycemia in newborn
D Macrosomia
Current answer: A) Intrauterine growth restriction (IUGR)
Justification

* IUGR is NOT a complication of diabetes — diabetes causes macrosomia, not IUGR. Hyperglycaemia in the newborn (neonatal hypoglycaemia) IS a complication.

Likely OKquizzes_q234Quizzes#234

A Gravida 1, para 0+0 woman, attends antenatal clinic for the booking visit at 12 weeks. Her blood group is B Rhesus negative. What is the next step in management?

A Perform Rhesus antibody titre
B Determine the husband's blood group
C Perform ultrasound scan
D Determine fetal blood group
Current answer: A) Perform Rhesus antibody titre
Justification

* At booking (12w), all Rh-negative women should have an antibody screen (indirect Coombs test) to check for existing Rh antibodies.

Likely OKquizzes_q235Quizzes#235

Techniques of delivery of aftercoming head in breech presentation include all EXCEPT

A Piper's Forceps
B External cephalic version
C Burns Marshall's technique
D Jaw flexion shoulder traction
Current answer: B) External cephalic version
Justification

* ECV is performed before labor to turn a breech to vertex; it is not a technique for delivering the aftercoming head. Piper forceps, Burns-Marshall, and jaw-flexion shoulder traction are for the aftercoming head.

Likely OKquizzes_q237Quizzes#237

A woman undergoes induction of labor with amniotomy followed by oxytocin infusion at a period of amenorrhea 42 weeks. Six hours later she is getting 4 contractions per 10 minutes and the fetal heart rate drops to 100 per minute. What is the first step in management?

A Stop oxytocin infusion
B Perform cardiotocograph
C Change the position of the mother
D Administer oxygen to the mother
Current answer: A) Stop oxytocin infusion
Justification

* Hyperstimulation (4 contractions/10 min) causing fetal bradycardia (100 bpm). First step: stop oxytocin to reduce uterine activity.

Likely OKquizzes_q238Quizzes#238

In which fetal presentation can vaginal delivery be expected?

A Shoulder presentation
B Transverse lie
C Brow presentation
D Face presentation when the chin lies under the symphysis pubis
Current answer: D) Face presentation when the chin lies under the symphysis pubis
Justification

* Mento-anterior (chin under pubis) face presentation can deliver vaginally. Transverse lie, shoulder, and brow cannot.

Likely OKquizzes_q239Quizzes#239

In pathological retraction ring: All are true EXCEPT

A Indicates obstructed labour and needs caesarean section
B Appears as a visible & palpable groove rising across the abdomen
C Can be felt vaginally
D More common in multiparous women
Current answer: D) More common in multiparous women
Justification

* Pathological retraction ring occurs in obstructed labor, which can occur in any parity. The other statements are true: it indicates obstructed labor (needs CS), appears as a visible/palpable groove, and can be felt vaginally.

Likely OKquizzes_q241Quizzes#241

Likely OKquizzes_q242Quizzes#242

Abnormal uterine action includes all of the following EXCEPT

A Constriction ring
B Cervical insufficiency
C Colicky uterus
D Pathological Retraction ring
E Cervical dystocia
Current answer: B) Cervical insufficiency
Justification

* Cervical insufficiency (incompetent cervix) is a structural cervical problem, not a type of abnormal uterine action. Constriction ring, colicky uterus, pathological retraction ring, and cervical dystocia are forms of abnormal uterine action.

Conflict reviewother_q1Other#1

Which of the following is NOT a component of premarital care?

A Genetic counselling
B Inter-conception care

D Promotion for proper ANC

Justification

Inter-conception care — Premarital care addresses couples before marriage; inter-conception care is between pregnancies, not a component of premarital care.

Likely OKother_q2Other#2

Which of the following is NOT included in the counseling sessions provided for the couples during premarital care?

A Genetic counselling
B Relevant health problem counselling
C Contraception counselling
D Healthy lifestyle change counselling
Current answer: C) Contraception counselling
Justification

Contraception counselling — Premarital care includes genetic, lifestyle, and health counselling; contraception counselling is typically after marriage.

Conflict reviewother_q3Other#3

Which of the following methods is the correct way to calculate the estimated date of delivery (EDD)?

A First day of last menstrual period (LMP) + 8 months + 1 week
B First day of last menstrual period (LMP) + 9 months
C Last day of last menstrual period (LMP) + 8 months + 1 week
D First day of last menstrual period (LMP) + 8 months
E First day of last menstrual period (LMP) + 9 months + 1 week
Current answer: A) First day of last menstrual period (LMP) + 8 months + 1 week
Why flagged: Duplicate question has conflicting keyed answers.
Conflict group: other_q3, other_q18, other_q96
Majority answer text: first day of last menstrual period lmp + 8 months + 1 week
Justification

First day of LMP + 8 months + 1 week — Naegele's rule: LMP + 7 days − 3 months. Equivalent to LMP + 9 months + 7 days (or + 8 months + 1 week in some textbooks).

Likely OKother_q4Other#4

A 38-year-old patient asks if she has to "hurry up" and get pregnant early after marriage. Which of the following is TRUE regarding her age?

A Advanced maternal age is not a contributor to infertility
B Preeclampsia and toxemia affect very young women and spare older women
C Diabetes mellitus is more common in advanced maternal age
D The incidence of fetal anomalies increases with age, whereas the number of abortions decreases
E As long as the patient is healthy, postponing pregnancy till age 40 is possible
Current answer: C) Diabetes mellitus is more common in advanced maternal age
Justification

Diabetes mellitus is more common in advanced maternal age — Older age is a risk factor for gestational and pre-existing diabetes.

Likely OKother_q5Other#5

During ANC for a 27-year-old primigravida with BMI 20, she is concerned about weight gain during pregnancy. What is the recommended weight gain?

A 7 to 11.5 kg
B 12.5 to 18 kg
C 11.5 to 16 kg
D 5 to 9 kg
E None of the above
Current answer: C) 11.5 to 16 kg
Justification

11.5 to 16 kg — IOM guidelines for normal BMI (18.5–24.9): 11.5–16 kg total weight gain.

Conflict reviewother_q6Other#6

B 16–18 weeks
C 18–20 weeks
D 22–24 weeks
Current answer: B) 16–18 weeks
Why flagged: Duplicate question has conflicting keyed answers.
Conflict group: other_q6, other_q21, other_q103
Justification

16–18 weeks — Multiparous women typically feel quickening at 16–18 weeks (primigravida at 18–20 weeks).

Likely OKother_q7Other#7

You are caring for a 33-year-old woman, 12 weeks pregnant, who smokes 2–3 cigarettes/day and drinks about 3 cups of alcohol per week. Which of the following is TRUE?

A Limit alcohol intake to one or two glasses of wine is advisable
B Children with fetal alcohol syndrome have developmental, behavioral, and learning problems
C Alcohol consumption in the third trimester is not directly associated with fetal alcohol syndrome
D Smoking must be discontinued at least 3 months before pregnancy to avoid its hazards
Current answer: B) Children with fetal alcohol syndrome have developmental, behavioral, and learning problems
Justification

Children with fetal alcohol syndrome have developmental, behavioral, and learning problems — FAS is associated with significant neurodevelopmental impairments; complete abstinence is recommended in pregnancy.

Likely OKother_q8Other#8

A 22-year-old primigravida with no psychiatric history delivers a healthy baby at 41 weeks and develops insomnia, anxiety, and crying spells on postpartum day 4. What is the most likely diagnosis?

A Postpartum blues
B Postpartum depression (PPD)
C Postpartum psychosis
D Alcohol withdrawal
Current answer: A) Postpartum blues
Justification

Postpartum blues — Onset day 3–5 postpartum with insomnia, anxiety, crying spells; self-limited and distinct from PPD/psychosis.

Likely OKother_q9Other#9

All of the following are risk factors for PPD EXCEPT

A Prior psychiatric disease of any kind
B The postpartum blues
C Cesarean section
D Exclusive bottle feeding
E Infant with special needs
Current answer: C) Cesarean section
Justification

Cesarean section — Prior C-section is a significant risk factor for PPD (postpartum depression) due to surgical recovery, pain, and potential bonding difficulties.

Likely OKother_q10Other#10

A pregnant lady came for her ANC visit at 24 weeks of gestation. What is the timing for her next visit according to the schedule of the minimum eight ANC visits?

A 26–30 weeks
B At least three weeks after the first visit
C 30–34 weeks
D At 36 weeks
E The next visit is scheduled according to patient availability
Current answer: A) 26–30 weeks
Justification

26–30 weeks — WHO eight-visit schedule: visits at <12, 20, 26, 30, 34, 36, 38, 40 weeks; at 24 weeks the next scheduled visit is 26 weeks (falls in 26–30 range).

Likely OKother_q11Other#11

A 29-year-old healthy, nulliparous woman stopped using OCPs recently and now depends inconsistently on condoms and withdrawal. She smokes one pack/day and her BMI is 31. Which of the following statements is NOT true regarding the use of combined hormonal contraceptives for this patient?

A It is associated with a significantly decreased risk of PID
B History of chlamydia is not a contraindication for hormonal contraception
C Decreases her risk of endometrial cancer
D It is contraindicated because of her obesity
E Estrogen may improve her acne
Current answer: D) It is contraindicated because of her obesity
Justification

It is contraindicated because of her obesity — BMI 31 alone is not an absolute contraindication for CHC (WHO MEC category 2 for BMI 30–34).

Likely OKother_q12Other#12

Your patient wants a reliable birth control method that does not require daily use or use during every sexual encounter. Her examination is completely normal. Which would be the most appropriate contraceptive method for this patient at this time?

A Bilateral tubal ligation (BTL) or vasectomy
B Vaginal contraceptive ring
C Progesterone-only pills
D A levonorgestrel IUD or Depo-Provera
Current answer: D) A levonorgestrel IUD or Depo-Provera
Justification

A levonorgestrel IUD or Depo-Provera — Both are long-acting, require no daily or coital action, and are highly effective.

Likely OKother_q13Other#13

You started a 20-year-old woman on COCs 2 months ago. She returns asking to discontinue them due to side effects. Which of the following is the most frequent reason for discontinuing COCs?

A Irregular bleeding
B Breast tenderness
C Fluid retention
D Headache
E Nausea
Current answer: A) Irregular bleeding
Justification

Irregular bleeding — Breakthrough bleeding/spotting is the most common reason for discontinuing COCs.

Likely OKother_q14Other#14

Which of the following statements would NOT be regarded as reasonable nutritional advice for a pregnant woman?

A Supplementation with calcium (1000–1300 mg per day)
B Supplementation with iron, especially if anemia is detected
C Supplementation with folic acid 0.4 mg daily throughout pregnancy
D Supplementation with vitamin A
E Supplementation with vitamin D if sunlight exposure is limited
Current answer: D) Supplementation with vitamin A
Justification

Supplementation with vitamin A — High-dose vitamin A is teratogenic; routine supplementation is not recommended in pregnancy.

Reviewother_q15Other#15

A 42-year-old pregnant woman is now 17 weeks and missed her combined test for Down's syndrome screening. You counsel her about the quadruple test. Which of the following is/are TRUE about the quad test?

B Unconjugated oestradiol, ACHT, AFP, and inhibin A
C Beta hCG, AFP, nuchal translucency, and inhibin A

Justification

Can be done from 15–20 weeks of gestation — Quad test (AFP, hCG, uE3, inhibin A) window is 15–20 weeks, optimally 16–18 weeks.

Likely OKother_q19Other#19

The primary purpose of prenatal care is to ensure a better outcome; a healthy baby and minimal risk to the mother. Which of the following is not important to achieve this objective?

A Early, accurate assessment of gestational age
B Regular visits focusing on health and well-being of the mother
C Identification of patients at risk (e.g., gestational diabetes, hypertension)
D Performance of anatomic ultrasound in the second trimester
E Attention to emotional health of the patient and family
Current answer: D) Performance of anatomic ultrasound in the second trimester
Justification

Performance of anatomic ultrasound in the second trimester — While valuable, anatomic ultrasound is not essential for the basic objective of a healthy mother and baby; the other listed elements are core ANC components.

Likely OKother_q20Other#20

During the physical examination for a pregnant woman during ANC, which of the following findings increases the risk of the pregnancy?

A Elevated blood pressure
B A retroverted uterus
C A thrombosed hemorrhoid
D Obesity
E Detected murmur of mitral stenosis
Current answer: E) Detected murmur of mitral stenosis
Justification

Detected murmur of mitral stenosis — Mitral stenosis is a significant cardiac lesion that increases pregnancy risk (pulmonary congestion, arrhythmias, maternal mortality).

Conflict reviewother_q21Other#21

Likely OKother_q22Other#22

A 38-year-old woman asks if she should hurry up and get pregnant after marriage. Which of the following is true regarding her age?

A Advanced maternal age is not a contributor to infertility
B Preeclampsia and toxemia affect very young, usually nulliparous women and spare older women

D The incidence of fetal anomalies increases with age, whereas the number of abortions decreases

Current answer: C) Diabetes mellitus is more commonly found in advanced maternal age

Diabetes mellitus is more commonly found in advanced maternal age — Increased age is associated with higher risk of GDM and type 2 diabetes.

Likely OKother_q23Other#23

Which of the following statements regarding alcohol consumption in pregnancy and fetal alcohol syndrome is/are true?

D Alcohol consumption in the third trimester is not directly associated with fetal alcohol syndrome

Justification

Abstain completely from alcohol during pregnancy — There is no known safe level of alcohol in pregnancy; complete abstinence is recommended.

Likely OKother_q24Other#24

B 12 gm/dl

Current answer: B) 12 gm/dl
Justification

12 gm/dl — Normal first-trimester hemoglobin is ≥11 g/dL; 12 g/dL is within normal range.

Likely OKother_q25Other#25

A 24-year-old woman presents for an initial prenatal visit. She is at 9 weeks' gestation based on her LMP. She had a previous pregnancy affected by a neural tube defect. What is the most likely prescription regarding folic acid?

A 4 g of folic acid once per day to be started at the first trimester of pregnancy
B 5 mg of folic acid once per day beginning at least one month prior to conception and continuing through the first trimester
C 0.6 mg folic acid once per day beginning at least one month prior to conception and continuing through pregnancy
Current answer: B) 5 mg of folic acid once per day beginning at least one month prior to conception and continuing through the first trimester
Justification

5 mg of folic acid once per day beginning at least one month prior to conception and continuing through the first trimester — High-dose (4–5 mg) folic acid is recommended for women with a previous NTD-affected pregnancy.

Likely OKother_q26Other#26

You are caring for a 15-year-old primiparous patient at 8 weeks' gestation. The patient is 164 cm tall and weighs 50 kg. The patient asks why she is supposed to gain so much weight. What is the best response?

A Gaining 8–12 kg is recommended for healthy fetal growth.
B Inadequate weight gain delays lactation after delivery.
C Weight gain is important to assure that you get enough vitamins.
D Inadequate weight gain can lead to decreased fetal growth and development.
Current answer: D) Inadequate weight gain can lead to decreased fetal growth and development.
Justification

Inadequate weight gain can lead to decreased fetal growth and development — This is the most accurate and evidence-based response about the importance of weight gain.

Likely OKother_q27Other#27

Women with inadequate weight gain during pregnancy are at higher risk of giving birth to an infant with

A Spina bifida
B Intrauterine growth restriction
C Diabetes mellitus
D Down syndrome
Current answer: B) Intrauterine growth restriction
Justification

Intrauterine growth restriction — Inadequate gestational weight gain is associated with IUGR and low birth weight.

Likely OKother_q28Other#28

The purpose of a nutritional assessment does not include which of the following?

A Identify individuals who are malnourished
B Provide data for designing a nutrition plan of care that will prevent or minimize the development of malnutrition
C Identify those who are at risk for malnutrition
D Establish a baseline data for evaluating the efficacy of nutritional care
E None of the above
Current answer: E) None of the above
Justification

None of the above — All listed options (identify malnutrition, provide data for nutrition plan, identify at-risk individuals, establish baseline) are purposes of nutritional assessment.

Likely OKother_q29Other#29

Which is not true regarding the comprehensive nutritional assessment?

A Is performed for all patients
B Includes dietary history and clinical information
C Includes physical examination
D Includes anthropometric measures
E Includes laboratory tests
Current answer: A) Is performed for all patients
Justification

Is performed for all patients — Comprehensive nutritional assessment is targeted at at-risk or malnourished patients, not all patients.

Likely OKother_q64Other#64

Current answer: B) Hematoma formation is less likely with the lower segment CS
Justification

Hematoma formation is less likely with the lower segment CS — The lower segment has better hemostasis, less tension on the incision, and heals with a stronger scar than the upper segment.

Justification

Safer in the presence of pelvic adhesions — Classical CS allows entry into the uterus above the level of adhesions, making it safer when dense pelvic adhesions are present.

B Ventilation
C PO₂
D Residual volume
E pH
Current answer: D) Residual volume
Justification

Justification

Increase in heart rate — Heart rate increases by 10–20 bpm in pregnancy, contributing to increased cardiac output (along with increased stroke volume).

Likely OKother_q138Other#138

More common in multiparous women — Pathological retraction ring (Bandl's ring) is more common in obstructed labor, especially in primigravidas, not multiparas.

Current answer: D) Carry out balloon tamponade
Justification

Likely OKother_q196Other#196

Justification

Justification

Termination of pregnancy by cesarean section after starting IV — Severe preeclampsia with features of HELLP (thrombocytopenia, elevated LFTs, headache, epigastric pain) at 35 weeks requires delivery after magnesium sulfate and antihypertensives.

Conflict reviewfinal_41_mcq1Final 41#1

The following are predisposing factors for placenta previa, except

A Advancing maternal age
B High older pregnancy
C Previous caesrean section
D Malpresentsation
E D&C
Current answer: D) Malpresentsation
Why flagged: Duplicate question has conflicting keyed answers. answerText does not cleanly match selected option.
Conflict group: exams_q90, final_41_mcq1
Justification

Advancing age, high parity, previous CS, and D&C are predisposing factors. Malpresentation is a **consequence** (the placenta occupies the lower uterine segment, preventing head engagement), not a cause. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq2Final 41#2

The following statements are related to cord prolapse except

A Common in malpresentation
B A loop of cord may be brought down to confirm the pulsation
C One of the first aid management is to lift the presenting part of the cord
D High risk of fetal distress
E Treated by emergency CS
Current answer: B) A loop of cord may be brought down to confirm the pulsation
Justification

Bringing the cord further down induces vasospasm. Pulsation should be checked where it presents without traction. Lifting the presenting part relieves compression. Emergency CS is the definitive treatment. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq3Final 41#3

A 39 year-old pregnant lady with gestational age of 38 weeks. CTG monitoring revealed transient decrease in fetal heart rate after beginning of uterine contractions and subsided after ending of the contractions. What is your diagnosis?

A Early deceleration
B Late deceleration
C Variable deceleration
D Sustained deceleration
E Bradycardia
Current answer: A) Early deceleration
Justification

The description matches the classical pattern of early deceleration: gradual onset, mirror image of the contraction, nadir at the peak, return to baseline by end of contraction. Benign, caused by head compression. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq4Final 41#4

The oxytocin infusion is contraindicated in all except

A Obstructed labour
B Trial of labour
C Hypovolamic shock
D Cardiac disease
E Cephalo-pelvic disproportion
Current answer: B) Trial of labour
Justification

Trial of labour (for borderline CPD or VBAC) is an indication for oxytocin augmentation. Obstructed labour, hypovolaemic shock, severe cardiac disease, and absolute CPD are contraindications. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq5Final 41#5

A 25 year-old pregnant lady at 39 weeks of gestation. During labor, a hand is found beside the head in cephalic presentation. What is your diagnosis?

A Occipito-anterior position
B occipito-posterior position
C Face Presentation
D Brow presentation
E Compound presentation
Current answer: E) Compound presentation
Justification

Compound presentation = a fetal extremity (hand/arm) prolapses alongside the presenting part (head/breech). Not O-A, O-P, face, or brow. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq6Final 41#6

What is the station when the fetal head is of the level of chin spines

A -2
B +2
C +1
D 0
E -1

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq7Final 41#7

Vaginal examination during pregnancy is contraindicated in

A Carcinoma of the cervix
B Gonorrhea
C Prolapsed cord
D Placenta previa
E Active labor with ruptured membranes >6 hours
Current answer: D) Placenta previa
Justification

Vaginal examination (digital) can provoke catastrophic haemorrhage in placenta previa. Sterile speculum exam for diagnosis is permissible in controlled theatre settings. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq8Final 41#8

All of the following are components of biophysical profile except

A Oxytocin challenge test
B Non stress test
C Amniotic fluid volume
D Fetal respiratory movement
E Fetal body movement
Current answer: A) Oxytocin challenge test
Justification

BPP components: NST + fetal breathing movements + fetal body movements + fetal tone + amniotic fluid volume (Manning criteria). OCT is a separate test (contraction stress test). |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq9Final 41#9

A Lower abdominal pain
B Vaginal bleeding
C Amenorrhea
D Shoulder pain
E Shock

Abdominal/pelvic pain is the most constant symptom of undisturbed ectopic pregnancy (present in ~95%), while amenorrhea may be absent or unnoticed. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq10Final 41#10

A woman with 20 weeks gestation presents with vaginal bleeding. On speculum examination, the cervical os is open but no products have come out. The diagnosis is

A Inevitable abortion
B Missed abortion
C Complete abortion
D Incomplete abortion
E Threatened abortion
Current answer: A) Inevitable abortion
Justification

Open os + bleeding without tissue passage = inevitable abortion. Product will pass soon. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq11Final 41#11

With reference to fetal heart rates, a non-stress test is considered reactive when

A Two fetal heart rate accelerations are noted in 20 minutes
B One fetal heart rate acceleration is noted in 20 minutes
C Two fetal heart rate accelerations are noted in 10 minutes
D Three heart rate accelerations are noted in 30 minutes
E Fetal heart accelerations during uterine contractions
Current answer: A) Two fetal heart rate accelerations are noted in 20 minutes
Justification

A reactive NST = ≥2 accelerations (≥15 bpm × ≥15 sec) in a 20-minute window. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq12Final 41#12

A 28 years old female with history of 8 weeks amenorrhea complains of vaginal bleeding and lower abdominal pain. On ultrasound examination there is gestational sac with absent fetal pulsation. The diagnosis is

A Inevitable abortion
B Missed abortion
C Threatened abortion
D Incomplete abortion
E Vesicular mole
Current answer: B) Missed abortion
Justification

Gestational sac present with absent fetal cardiac activity (fetal death in utero) = missed abortion (missed miscarriage). The conceptus is retained but non-viable. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq13Final 41#13

Ligation of which artery can control postpartum hemorrhage?

A Origin of internal iliac artery
B Anterior division of internal iliac artery
C Posterior division of internal iliac artery
D Common iliac artery
E External iliac artery
Current answer: B) Anterior division of internal iliac artery
Justification

Hypogastric (internal iliac) artery ligation reduces pulse pressure. The anterior division gives off the uterine, vaginal, and superior vesical arteries. The posterior division supplies the gluteal region. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq14Final 41#14

Folic acid supplement reduces the risk of

A Anencephaly
B Preeclampsia
C Down syndrome
D Placenta previa
E Congenital heart disease
Current answer: A) Anencephaly
Justification

Periconceptional folic acid reduces neural tube defects (anencephaly, spina bifida, encephalocele) by up to 70%. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq15Final 41#15

Preterm premature rupture of the membranes is most strictly defined as spontaneous rupture at any time prior to which of the following

A Fetal viability
B Second stage of labor
C The 32nd week of gestation
D Onset of labor
E The 37th week of gestation
Current answer: E) The 37th week of gestation
Justification

PROM = rupture before onset of labour. PPROM = PROM **before 37 completed weeks** (preterm). |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq16Final 41#16

First trimester abortion is mostly due to

A Cervical insufficiency
B Aneuploidy
C Toxoplasma
D Trauma
E Uterine septum
Current answer: B) Aneuploidy
Justification

Chromosomal abnormalities account for ~50–60% of first-trimester spontaneous losses (trisomy 16, monosomy X, triploidy). |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq17Final 41#17

Misoprostol has been found to be effective in all of the following except

A Missed abortion
B Induction of labour
C Menorrhagia
D Prevention of postpartum haemorrhage
E Management of atonic postpartum haemorrhage
Current answer: C) Menorrhagia
Justification

Misoprostol (PGE1 analogue) is effective for: missed abortion (evacuation), induction of labour, prevention of PPH, and treatment of atonic PPH. Not a standard treatment for menorrhagia. |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq18Final 41#18

A woman complains of sudden onset of watery vaginal discharge at a period of amenorrhea 32 weeks. What is the first step in management?

A Commence broad spectrum antibiotics
B Commence corticosteroids
C Perform sterile speculum examination
D Perform ultrasound san to assess Amniotic fluid volume
E Commence tocolysis
Current answer: C) Perform sterile speculum examination
Justification

Sterile speculum exam confirms ROM (pooling, fern test, Nitrazine test). It avoids introducing infection and guides subsequent management (antibiotics, corticosteroids, tocolysis as indicated). |

Likely OKfinal_41_mcq19Final 41#19

Variable deceleration denotes

A Head compression
B Cord compression
C Placental insufficiency
D Fetal distress
E Head descent
Current answer: B) Cord compression
Justification

Variable decelerations are caused by umbilical cord compression (abrupt onset, variable shape, rapid return). |

Conflict reviewfinal_41_mcq20Final 41#20

Ventouse in second stage of labor is contraindicated in

A Persistent occipto posterior position
B Heart disease
C Uterine inertia
D Preterm labor
E Fetal distress
Current answer: D) Preterm labor
Why flagged: Duplicate question has conflicting keyed answers.
Conflict group: exams_q151, quizzes_q34, quizzes_q80, quizzes_q148, quizzes_q246, other_q127, final_41_mcq20
Majority answer text: preterm labor
Justification

Vacuum is contraindicated in preterm (<34–36 wks) because the fetal skull is soft and fragile (high risk of cephalohematoma, intracranial haemorrhage, subgaleal haemorrhage). |
